Both arms use complete-response CE; one adds an exact full-vocabulary KL penalty against our existing general/code routes. **All-four-L40capacity/recovery passed and both coding arms have started production training; no new held coding gain is established.** See [full-size proof](reports/own-aligned-assistant-v3-full-CPU-v1/complete.json), [independent readback](reports/own-aligned-assistant-v3-full-CPU-v1/independent-complete-readback.json) and [live queue](reports/code-initialized-alignment-v1-queue.json).

The preceding full human SFT experiment completed three **3,072-update** arms on every **12,287 training conversation**. Across all **677 held conversations / 292,093 response/EOS targets**, the selected arm reduces token NLL **2.494 → 2.049** and improves teacher-forced token accuracy **50.1% → 56.4%**. Generated knowledge instead regresses **13/559 → 1/559**, while choice-likelihood correctness changes **74/559 → 76/559**. Local completion syntax changes **18/24 → 16/24**, instruction syntax **20/24 → 6/24**; functional and efficiency passes remain **zero** in both formats. C++ syntax/function/efficiency remain **0/12**. Native OCR exact matches change **0/170 → 1/170**, but character error worsens **0.946 → 2.380**; proper-image object matches **46/164 → 48/164** remain below wrong-image matches **50/164 → 58/164**. Supported public-program finite-case passes change **1/175 → 2/175**, with syntax **143/175 → 106/175**. An independent reader reexecutes all three delivered passing programs on every original case; one problem is gained and the earlier pass is retained. The completed full math comparison regresses **19/1,319 → 6/1,319**: one earlier correct answer is retained, five gained and18lost. Every original question/raw token reconciles and the candidate remains unpromoted. The completed CPU/FP32 plain/chat diagnostic preserves every original question and token: initial generated correctness is **13/559 plain and 22/559 chat**, versus trained **1/559 plain and 8/559 chat**. Framing helps each policy, but regression remains within both matched frames. This post-hoc result preserves the original CUDA/BF16 benchmark. Full human-ranked reward-head learning has now completed, with held-pair accuracy51.1%to55.6%; fresh own-policy PPO and useful genuine-human alignment are unfinished. **No candidate is promoted and high-end capability is not achieved.** See [training readback](reports/whole-human-SFT-v1/independent-complete-training-readback-v1.json), [held-target readback](reports/whole-human-SFT-v1/independent-complete-held-targets-v1.json) and [native knowledge readback](reports/whole-human-SFT-v1/independent-native-knowledge-readback-v1.json) and [complete public-program readback](reports/whole-human-SFT-v1/independent-public-comparison-v1.json).

The complete shared word pack is independently read back and admitted: **91,585 training crops / 3,429 held crops**, including **41,585 human-labelled real training word crops** and **50,000 rendered training examples**. Every original transcript, fixed-geometry pixel transform, native UTF-8/CTC target, whole BPE response/EOS mask and retained role separation is checked. This counts crops, not independent photographs. The new shared trainer couples whole-word byte CTC to image-conditioned native response CE, updating only **2,714,178 vision/word values in 211 tensors**. Its three focused tests and **187.99-second actual full-size CPU proof** pass: two disposable updates, exact next-update weights/Adam/RNG recovery, unchanged original routes and aligned text-only output, and exact trained image/word cold reload. An independent reader checks both retained optimizer states, all unchanged tensors and every used original pixel/target. A full three-rate comparison is now an actual source-bound queue after the existing coding experiment: **5,725 updates per arm**, each training on every **91,585 crop once**, with an unrepeated one-crop final batch. It will evaluate every **3,429 held crop** with proper/blank/within-family wrong images, retaining both byte-CTC paths and native greedy tokens, and compare the original OCR/object/caption suites. **Production word updates have not started and held reading improvement is unproven; crops alone do not locate or read text throughout a full photo.** The vision experiment uses the frozen original own code anchor; attachment to the later mixed-language policy still needs separate final integration and evaluation. See [train-only admission](data/shared-byte-words-v1/admission.json), [independent all-source readback](data/shared-byte-words-v1/independent-source-readback.json) and [word trainer](src/slm_lab/shared_word_stage_v1.py), [actual full-size proof](reports/shared-words-full-CPU-v1/complete.json) and [independent probe readback](reports/shared-words-full-CPU-v1/independent-complete-readback.json). The [current full-scope review](reports/shared-word-production-queue-and-framing-diagnostic-v1-review.json) records live jobs, evidence and remaining requirements.

Earlier assembled candidate: the trained text/code/grounding components load with **one shared original backbone and 986,450,944 unique parameters**. The reference manifest loads the completed 815-update own code components and 1,137-update head without loading an external policy or duplicating the backbone. The full CPU/FP32 validation reproduces general/code image generation, trained grounding logits and a pre-existing code cache exactly; all original/code bytes and original pointers/versions stay unchanged. This proves assembly and route parity. The candidate is **experimental and not promoted**. See the [candidate manifest](checkpoints/own-assistant-candidate-v1/manifest.json) and [actual trained composite validation](reports/own-assistant-candidate-v1/validation-complete.json).

The completed coding experiment gives a mixed result: local completion syntax **11 → 20/24**, public-subset syntax **130 → 132/175**, but public whole-program finite-case passes **5 → 4/175** and local functional/empirical-efficiency passes remain **0/24** in both prompt formats. All five earlier public passing IDs are lost. General-domain and native image outputs are retained. The original final report failed after training/evaluation because it omitted the 559 original domain rows; a separate CPU reviewer supplies those rows to the unchanged original reader, reconstructs the complete source/state/results, and preserves the original failure. No additional model update follows from that repair. See [independently repaired endpoint](reports/own-code-generation-repair-endpoint-v2/complete.json).

The completed **head-only** experiment improves held attention-cell hits **2.86% → 5.77%** and query-mean soft-target CE **5.046 → 4.933** on all **2,027 original held queries**. Correct image/query CE beats the declared controls with positive paired-photo intervals. Its top-cell hit rate remains below the **6.96% training position prior**. Every original native OCR/object/caption output and score stays exact. This is approximate human mouse attention, not object-box/OCR accuracy or general visual reasoning. See [controlled and native results](reports/frozen-base-pointer-v1/matched-attention-and-native-comparison.json).

The shared candidate has also run on **all four L40s in CUDA/BF16**: all three trained routes are finite, both generation routes match their component outputs, and grounding preserves a pre-existing code cache. A fixed train-only decoding pilot using the own code/general likelihood difference fails its predeclared selection criterion: normal greedy generation passes **1/16** original training problems with **16/16** syntactically valid programs; contrast strengths 0.5 and 1.0 pass **0/16**, with **8/16 and 7/16** syntactically valid. An independent CPU reviewer reexecutes **all 48 delivered programs** on their complete original cases and reproduces all raw outcomes and qualification decisions. No decoder setting is selected or promoted. These are train-calibration results, not held accuracy or RLHF. See [pilot comparison](reports/own-view-contrastive-v1/comparison.json), [complete independent reexecution](reports/own-view-contrastive-v1-readback/complete.json), and [current full-scope review](reports/shared-assistant-and-decoding-v1-review.json).

A completed coding investigation separates question conditioning from free generation. Across **288 complete response/condition/route scores** on 16 original training programs, the own code view gives every matching question uniquely better likelihood than the other seven questions with the same interface. Its proper token NLL is **0.801**, with **78.37% teacher-forced argmax accuracy**; greedy case passes on these programs remain **1/16**. This is training-source likelihood, not held correctness. A separate fixed **64-problem** native greedy collection passes **4/64** complete finite case sets, with 30 complete wrong answers, 27 runtime qualifications and three compile failures. **28 complete wrong-value own responses** independently reexecute and pair with unchanged passing human programs: **3,115 human response/EOS targets and 1,494 own-negative targets**. Genuine EOS, whole prompts/masks and source roles are checked; truncation/runtime-qualified negatives stay outside admission. These are machine execution preferences. Their completed matched CE/execution-DPO follow-up is reported below. See [conditioning and actual own-feedback evidence](reports/own-conditioning-and-onpolicy-feedback-v1-review.json).

The matched **128-update** CE/execution-DPO experiment is complete. Both actual **986,450,944-parameter** models pass four-L40 capacity/exact recovery and trained one-backbone text/code/grounding reload. The full independent endpoint checks every code tensor, all **386 Adam states**, source draw/target/mask/cursor, DPO term and RNG payload. On all **175 supported public development problems**, whole-program finite-case passes are **4 initially, 8 with CE, 7 with added execution DPO**; every candidate pass reexecutes on all original cases. CE completion/instruction syntax is **21/24 and20/24**, versus DPO **22/24 in both**. **Local functional/efficiency remains zero in both formats, and C++ syntax regresses from1/12to0/12; C++functional/efficiency remainszero. Neither candidate is promoted.** Original general/vision/critic/head bytes, versions and native probes stay fixed. The original waiter is restored and every registered experiment actor is terminal. See [complete independent comparison](reports/own-onpolicy-pilot-v2/independent-comparison.json) and [current full-scope review](reports/completed-execution-pilot-and-education-budget-v1-review.json).

Additional foundation data is now packed: the complete pinned CommonPile LibreTexts blob contains **40,049 pages / 782 books / 360,758,933 text bytes** across mathematics, medicine, chemistry, biology, physics, humanities, social sciences and other fields. Seven empty texts account for the earlier six exact duplicate counts; there are no duplicated nonempty whole texts. Whole-book splits retain **35,470 train pages / 704 books / 82,776,507 own-BPE tokens** and **4,572 held pages / 78 books / 9,291,702 tokens**. Independent raw-source/token/window readback covers every selected page and all final next-token targets, including padded tails. The compatible retained hash indexes have no whole-text/body/code variant matches; this does not certify novelty against all earlier sources or semantic independence. **The pack is now admitted for the original training role; full-corpus production training has not started.** A separate [admission](data/open-education-bookpack-v1/admission.json) and [metadata readback](data/open-education-bookpack-v1/admission-readback.json) supersede the unchanged historical preparer flag. All 782 book attribution ledgers retain literal publisher values and missing fields. Original metadata retains **7,228 missing/empty authors, four missing titles and 757 unversioned GFDL declarations** for review; no attribution is invented. Most material has Creative Commons or GFDL declarations and is not described as public domain. See [packed corpus](data/open-education-bookpack-v1/corpus.json), [independent full readback](data/open-education-bookpack-v1/independent-raw-source-and-token-readback.json) and [limited retained-index audit](data/open-education-bookpack-v1/cross-corpus-hash-index-review.json).

The alignment/vision architecture has **999,860,418 actual unique parameters**. It shares the existing own backbone and original vocabulary table; the new language/vision factors, norms, reward, critic and word head add **13,409,474 parameters**. Its initial six focused CPU checks and full-model disposable probe cover cache/route isolation, complete byte CTC, sampling through the separate critic, new-only serialization and corrupt Adam/RNG/source-cursor rejection before mutation. All **599 selected Adam states**, next-update metrics, new weights and RNG recover exactly; a fresh extension wrapper reloads every new tensor and reproduces aligned/word outputs. Original general/code/grounding tensor bytes, versions, sampled native image-route outputs and pre-existing code caches stay exact. HH ranking responses retain their **other-model-generated, human-ranked** provenance. The separate PPO critic remains untrained. Later GPU capacity and full human SFT results are described above and below; this initial probe is a mechanics check. See [initial full-model probe](reports/own-aligned-assistant-full-CPU-v1/complete.json) and [independent source/tensor readback](reports/own-aligned-assistant-full-CPU-v1/independent-extension-and-source-readback.json).

A scan of the v1 word head found that its maximum **28 CTC frames** cannot fit **18 of 91,585 existing training transcripts** or **one of 3,429 held transcripts**, including repeated-byte alignment. Training sources include 50,000 rendered words and 41,585 human-labelled real word crops. Their complete original transcripts need up to **56 training / 36 held frames**. The revised v2 computation repeats patch features before the recurrent head, giving **140 CTC steps without adding parameters**; even 64 identical bytes fit without truncation. It adds no pixel detail or evidence of reading accuracy. Its distinct export format prevents loading it as the older computation. The word-crop head does not locate text in full photos. See [original CTC capacity finding](reports/own-aligned-assistant-full-CPU-v1/whole-word-CTC-capacity.json) and [actual revised GPU checks](reports/own-alignment-four-L40-v2/complete.json).

The whole-response human curriculum is independently reconstructed and admitted: **12,287 reviewed human dialogue training examples / 677 held examples**, plus **85,786 human-ranked training pairs / 4,642 held pairs** from complete pinned original helpful-base/harmless-base files. The raw four HH blobs contain 86,372 train and 4,666 test records; every comparison is retained or explicitly reviewed. Replacing the legacy half-window cap recovers **302 complete training responses and 14 held responses**, preserving every earlier retained conversation example. The actual longest whole training context is **4,089 tokens**, with up to **4,032 response/EOS targets**. The reader checks every original human history/review/target, every original ranked pair including response whitespace, every native token/answer mask and protected source role. All original HH test prompt groups and named knowledge/math/code questions remain outside gradients; semantic independence and factual accuracy are not certified. HH responses remain other-model-generated and human-ranked. **Full conversation SFT has now run; ranked reward learning and policy alignment remain unfinished.** See [source manifest](data/whole-human-alignment-v2/manifest.json), [independent all-source readback](data/whole-human-alignment-v2/independent-source-readback.json) and [admission](data/whole-human-alignment-v2/admission.json).

The revised actual **999,860,418-parameter** model passes source-extreme capacity/restart checks on **all four L40s**, at **6,888,011,264 peak allocated bytes / 8,105,492,480 peak reserved bytes per GPU**. Each independent replica executes two logical disposable updates and three physical optimizer calls using both actual longest conversation controls, three longest ranking controls and the longest retained training word (50 UTF-8 bytes). Every selected new tensor has finite gradients, all **600 Adam states** and the next update recover exactly, and original weights/versions/native probes/code caches remain exact. The four saved new-weight/Adam/RNG states are byte-identical by independent tensor readback. CTC scalar reduction runs on the CPU with gradients returning to GPU parameters; the critic target is a disposable capacity scalar, **not an RLHF return**. Capacity weights were discarded before the fresh full human SFT run. That earlier lease was restored; the current native comparisons use a separate owned lease. **Ranked reward/DPO/PPO and useful generated-answer capability remain unproven; no candidate is promoted.** See [independent four-device readback](reports/own-alignment-four-L40-v2/independent-complete-readback.json) and [current review](reports/full-human-SFT-and-reward-learning-v1-review.json).

Completed full human-code run, **2026-10-16**: our own **706M** model finished the fixed **3,203 updates** on all four L40s, covering all **102,487 admitted human APPS programs**, nine rounding repeats and **29,489,610 response/EOS targets**, plus sampled rehearsal from 29 earlier families. All language parameters trained; vision and critic stayed frozen. The independent final readback now proves all332native-export tensors equal the saved model, all218language Adam states are finite at3,203steps, all4worker RNG payloads/cursors are retained, and every original code/rehearsal draw reconstructs. The complete own native export is retained. On all **12,068 APPS validation programs**, token NLL improves **0.892 → 0.584** and teacher-forced token accuracy **80.0% → 85.2%**. Native completion/instruction syntax improves **3 → 13/24** and **5 → 17/24**, but both still score **zero functional/efficiency passes**. Generated domain answers regress **5 → 1/559**. The supported public-Python subset improves **22 → 122/175 syntax** and **0 → 3/175 published-case passes**; this is not a standard full or certified independent MBPP score. C++ remains **1/12 syntax and 0/12 functional/efficiency passes**. The completed matched CUDA/BF16 math assessment improves **1 → 18/1,319** correct numeric answers (0.076% → 1.365%); this remains weak and does not prove the reasoning is valid. The same 706M candidate’s older CPU/FP32 run has now finished at **17/1,319**; every original ID/gold/context and delivered raw-response numeric score is read back. Its paired CPU baseline still runs. This score belongs to the 706M APPS checkpoint, with precision kept separate from CUDA/BF16 and from the new dense-growth/999M models. **The candidate is not promoted; high-end capability is unproven.** See the [sealed training plan](reports/apps-full-language-v1/plan.json), [complete training record](reports/apps-full-language-v1/training-complete.json), and [full source-validation comparison](reports/apps-full-language-v1/APPS-validation/comparison.json).

Broader coding checks are prepared separately. A declared stdlib/class/stdin runtime checked one source-selected human program per 4,473 admitted train problems: **3,548 passed all 17,990 published cases**, independently reconciled against original programs, case bytes and raw results. These are **source-reference checks, not model scores or human preferences**. All **5,000 original publisher test problems** are retained outside training; every source file and whole own-BPE prompt was independently reconstructed. The local protocol supports **4,931 complete problems / 103,985 cases**, with 69 explicit reviews. A fixed **86-problem** before/after diagnostic is complete: syntax **8 → 64/86**, but **zero whole-program case passes** for both checkpoints. All 172 original prompts, token deliveries, program compilation and complete raw case comparisons were independently reconstructed. The new policy completes 34 case sets and passes 34 individual cases across them, without passing any whole problem; incomplete/resource-qualified runs are preserved as qualifications. This diagnostic is not a full or certified independent APPS benchmark. See [execution, held-source and queued-diagnostic evidence](reports/apps-extended-execution-and-test-evaluation-v1-review.json).

Execution-backed debugging data is now **independently audited and admitted for a later supervised repair experiment**. All **10,641 presealed token edits** were executed against their complete original training cases. **6,520 full repair examples** cover **2,766 unchanged human programs / 1,007,623 response/EOS targets**; 17 longer examples remain outside training. Every source/edit/counterexample and whole own-BPE mask reconstructs, with zero matches in the named exact held-source screens. These screens do not certify semantic or prior-public-corpus independence. The actual packed reader and held-role rejection pass. **The separately sealed 815-update experiment is complete; public-subset whole-program passes fall from 5 to 4/175 and local functional scores remain zero. These are machine execution signals, not human preferences.** A complementary supervised generation pack now retains all **3,548 complete original human programs** that pass all **17,990 finite published training cases**, using their unchanged native generation prompts: **550,701 response/EOS targets**, maximum whole context 3,773. Every actual stored sequence, label mask, original source hash and raw source-case value reconstructs. The completed generation/repair experiment consumed this train-only pack. The input pack itself adds no neural updates. See [verified generation admission/readback](reports/APPS-verified-generation-v1/complete.json).

The full **1,319-question, 512-token matched math assessment** completed after the code owner and its registered evaluations: **1 → 18 correct**, with format compliance **6.90% → 79.30%**. It uses the original native prompts, cached greedy generation and exact rational final-answer parser in CUDA/BF16, distinct from the still-running CPU/FP32 protocol. Two focused checks verify exact small-model CPU reproduction of the original evaluator, full coverage rejection, and rejection of active original work before GPU queries/signals. Independent source accounting now covers the actual **2,048-update history**: **65,536 distinct human programs / 18,888,586 response/EOS targets**, plus every rehearsal draw, with 15,408 distinct rehearsal positions reencoded from source. This is a partial epoch and sampled older-source rehearsal. The eight native baseline assessments have been recounted from saved answers/test outcomes. The completed CPU endpoint reviewer checks full weights/Adam/RNG/cursors, exact native export equality, all source draws/targets and matched before/after code/domain/OCR/object/caption results. It preserves the historical checkpoint readback and adds no GPU load. Four focused accounting/fidelity checks pass. All eight matched native comparisons are available; syntax and APPS token prediction improve, public-Python case passes rise to3/175, local/86task functional code remains zero, and generated knowledge regresses. Complete endpoint state proof and all native suites are retained; matched full math is complete at **1 → 18/1,319**. See [exposure and endpoint evidence](reports/apps-exposure-and-native-endpoint-review-v1-readiness.json).

The original human-attention experiment is complete: **36,354 unique training queries / 1,137 updates**, with 30 rounding repeats. It uses **706,799,104 total / 52,410,880 trainable parameters** and leaves every language/critic byte unchanged. The corrected deterministic resize path passed all-four-L40 capacity; all 334 exported/resume tensors and 114 exact-step Adam states were independently reconciled with every original source draw and all four RNG payloads. On all **2,027 held queries**, observed attention-cell hits improve **2.86% → 8.24%** and query-mean CE improves **5.046 → 4.835**. Correct-image/query predictions outperform blank/wrong images, disjoint same-photo phrases and train-only position priors, with positive paired original-photo intervals. **This is approximate human attention, not object-box accuracy or general visual reasoning.** Native image answers regress: **46 → 5/164 object matches**, OCR exact **0 → 1/170** but character error worsens, and caption NLL **1.772 → 2.522**. **The new vision weights remain experimental and are not substituted into the general or coding model.** The frozen-original-base follow-up is complete, with 2.86% to 5.77% held attention-cell hits and exact native image-output retention; details appear above. See [complete neural/source readback](reports/point-grounding-joint-v2/complete-neural-source-readback.json) and [matched controlled/native results](reports/point-grounding-joint-v2/matched-attention-and-native-comparison.json).

The frozen-original-base visual experiment is **complete**: all **36,354 training queries / 1,137 updates**, with 30 rounding repeats and **786,432 trainable parameters**. All-four-L40 capacity and actual exact next-update CUDA/head/Adam recovery pass; both learned matrices, both exact-step Adam states, every original query/point/token/draw and all four RNG payloads reconstruct. Every original language/vision/critic tensor stays frozen. The completed head now participates in the actual **986,450,944-parameter** shared candidate described above. Four kernel/inference and three controller tests pass. A new conditional-code prototype initializes **our own learned weight differences**, using a shared frozen own general backbone and a separate code view. [LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.09685v2) establishes the low-rank update principle; [SVD-LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07378v5) also motivates checking outputs rather than assuming weight-space truncation preserves quality. Our baseline uses plain rank352SVD differences across168decoder matrices plus our own code embedding/norms; it does not reproduce SVD-LLM whitening or import external weights. The actual model has **985,664,512 unique parameters**, or **986,450,944 including the reserved future grounding head**. Every original general/vision/critic tensor remains byte-identical, and both routes share one backbone/vision encoder. Code-only updates preserve general text/image/point behavior in small-model tests; the explicit code view includes every unique general/code parameter. Five focused CPU checks pass, including reference-bound reload and stale/wrong-route cache rejection. The completed CPU compression retains72.964%matrix-delta energy. On **four complete training contexts /238response+EOS targets**, code-source argmax agreement is232/238; source NLL is1.633general,0.729full-code and0.780compressed, with0.0137KL. **These are source-calibration results, not held code correctness.** The complete **175-problem native CUDA/BF16 comparison** now measures **122 → 130 syntactically valid outputs** and **3 → 5 published-case passes**. Every new pass was re-executed on all original cases. All three earlier passing IDs are lost and five other IDs pass, so aggregate improvement does not establish per-problem retention. All four L40s passed actual longest-prompt cached inference checks. That comparison uses the compressed initialization before additional training. Its supervised generation/repair epoch is complete: public finite-case passes5to4/175 and local functional/efficiency scoreszero. Automatic routing and high-end capability remain unproven. See the [complete native code comparison](reports/own-code-delta-public-v1/comparison.json) and [current measured evidence](reports/math-code-and-grounding-recovery-v1-review.json).

The own conditional-code generation/repair epoch completed all **815 updates**: **6,520 generation draws / 6,520 unique repairs / 6,520 language-balanced CodeNet draws**. Generation covers all 3,548 verified original human programs plus 2,972 repeats; sampled CodeNet covers **54 languages**, 5,814 distinct original positions and 706 repeats. All **4,645,006 response/EOS targets** and **17,301,327 complete context tokens** reconstruct. The separate global target-token means have weights **1.0 / 0.5 / 0.25**. Only the 279,651,840 own code parameters train; all original general/vision/critic bytes remain exact. Complete source/export/resume/386-Adam-state/four-worker-RNG readback passes in the independent reviewer. Public correctness falls **5 → 4/175** despite syntax gains; local functional/efficiency scores remain zero. These are machine execution signals, not genuine-human preference RLHF. See [sealed experiment](reports/own-code-generation-repair-v1/plan.json), [actual capacity/recovery](reports/own-code-generation-repair-v1/capacity.json) and [complete independent comparison](reports/own-code-generation-repair-v1/independent-comparison.json).

Current architecture experiment, **2026-10-16**: two actual **857,007,616-parameter** models completed 128 updates and all fixed native assessments. Reallocating 23,592,960 code parameters from FFN residuals to rank-96 Q/K/V/O attention updates keeps the exact total parameter budget. Both variants use the same complete CodeNet, verified-program and rehearsal exposures with fresh Adam; neither uses a DPO gradient. Six architecture checks and one driver check pass. Independent readback verifies all adapter tensors, complete Adam/RNG/cursors, all 374 original reference scores and both actual 128-update checkpoints. **Functional correctness and efficiency remain 0/24 in both prompt formats for both models.** Completion/instruction syntax is 15/24 and 1/24 for FFN-only, versus 13/24 and 5/24 with attention. Generated knowledge is 2/559 versus 6/559, both below the joint base's 13/559; likelihood scores are 73/559 and 68/559. Lower source-validation NLL does not establish correct code. Neither candidate is promoted. All experiment workers and the watchdog ended, and the exact original image-data waiter resumed. This uses established [LoRA prior work](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.09685), not external weights or a novelty claim. See the [complete comparison](reports/attention-allocation-pilot-v1/independent-comparison.json) and [actual checkpoint readback](reports/attention-allocation-pilot-v1-readback.json).

An additional public source is ready for human-solution supervised training: **102,487 complete APPS programs / 29,487,086 response targets**, plus 12,068 internal-validation programs. The original publisher archive contains 5,000 training problems and 117,232 human solutions; all are accounted for as 114,560 whole candidates plus 2,672 preserved reviews. Five further exact normalized train/validation duplicates are excluded from training. An independent audit re-encodes every actual candidate input, program, EOS and mask against the original source. Named exact screens cover retained MBPP test references, 12 local Python references and Python3-parseable CodeNet validation/test programs, with zero matches; this does not certify semantic or prior-corpus independence. Original APPS test problems are not encoded for training. The source omits 195 training test files; no tests are invented, and the programs are **not execution-verified preferences or human RLHF**. The actual reader preserves complete responses up to 3,626 targets without the legacy half-window clip. **The later complete APPS human-code epoch has run; its measured results appear above.** This source supplements earlier required production and multimodal work. See [publisher APPS](https://github.com/hendrycks/apps), the [complete source audit](data/public-apps-complete-pack-v2/independent-source-audit.json), [human-CE admission](data/public-apps-human-solution-view-v1/admission.json) and [runtime reader evidence](reports/public-apps-human-solution-view-v1-readback.json).

Current work, **2026-10-16**: the original public-code continuation completed **936 updates / 245,306,880 target tokens**, and the original OS/network/security stage completed **1,138 updates / 298,247,040 target-token exposures**. The current frozen image stage has **56,237 examples**, covering ordinary photos, scene text, documents, object questions, scientific imagery and rendered text. Fashion-MNIST contributes only **500 clothing classification examples (0.89%)** and cannot establish generic visual understanding. An additional ordinary-photo corpus has **78,255 prepared captioned photos**; **23,847 additional scene-word crops** and **5,858 original human TextVQA questions**, each with a plain and an own-reader-conditioned input, are admitted to the later joint-training schedule. The original joint stage has finished; these additional inputs remain queued in the later continuation. See `reports/generic-image-input-status-v5.json` and the finalized dataset manifests for counts and source attribution.

The first sustained broad public-data pilot **completed 1,199 updates on four L40 GPUs**. Its 349.5M-token mixture contains 314.3M training tokens and 35.2M validation tokens; 68% of training tokens come from GitHub code/documentation and technical essays. Reference material spans all 15 requested domain groups and includes public-domain books. The subsequent multimodal pilot also completed training/evaluation. Current capability remains weak: functional code generation passed 0/24 local tasks, and image reading passed 1/170 held-out examples exactly. Human-feedback training/evaluation has completed; see `reports/human-feedback-pilot-v1/status.json`. Larger language/code collection and further visual experiments are in progress.

This is an unfinished research project. Training/evaluation records are tracked in `reports/`; a submitted or completed job is not proof of a capable model. Early CPU validation used a **136,224-parameter replica** and a freshly trained 512-token BPE tokenizer. Successful training steps verify mechanics; they do not establish general intelligence or parity with a high-end LLM.

The implementation includes a GQA/RoPE/SwiGLU decoder with tied token embeddings, a bidirectional patch vision encoder, pooled image tokens, causal text attention, gradient checkpointing, and generation. Training supports pretraining, multimodal supervised fine-tuning, DPO, human-preference reward modeling, and PPO-RLHF with a learned critic and a fixed reference policy. Pretraining, SFT, DPO, and reward training support PyTorch DDP; pretraining and SFT support gradient accumulation weighted by the global number of target tokens; PPO currently supports one device. Packed text uses causal SDPA without a quadratic padding mask, and CUDA attention uses native grouped KV heads where supported. Generation now uses a grouped KV cache with rotary position offsets and encodes an image once. Cached FP32 logits match full recomputation within numerical tolerance; BF16 kernel rounding can change greedy tokens. See `reports/l40-cache-benchmark.json` for the contended full-size check.

Public corpus preparation supports revision-pinned streaming, disk-backed document deduplication, document-separated train/validation splits, and memory-mapped token shards. Training checkpoints save the optimizer and RNG state, retain a bounded number of snapshots, and check the dataset, tokenizer, stage, hyperparameters, and worker count before resuming.

Use the isolated environment from this folder:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.hardware
```

To install in a fresh environment, install an appropriate PyTorch build from the [official selector](https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/), then install the project:

```bash
python -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -e '.[data,dev]'
```

This instance now exposes **four NVIDIA L40 GPUs**, each with 48GB nominal memory. The isolated environment uses CUDA-enabled PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130. All four GPUs passed BF16 computation and a full-size, four-worker public-text training check. The short check reached approximately 30,000 aggregate target tokens/second after warmup and used about 16.2GB peak allocated memory per GPU; sustained throughput may differ. The earlier A100 allocation problem is resolved.

A bounded public-data sample already exists in `data/public-sample/`. To collect a new sample into a new directory:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.data fetch --output data/new-sample
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.data tokenizer \
  --data data/new-sample/pretrain.train.jsonl \
  --output data/new-sample/tokenizer.json --vocab-size 32768
```

Samples come from [FineWeb-Edu](https://huggingface.co/datasets/HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu), the AI2D subset of [The Cauldron](https://huggingface.co/datasets/HuggingFaceM4/the_cauldron), and [Anthropic HH-RLHF](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Anthropic/hh-rlhf). HH-RLHF is used only for preference training. Downloaded examples and images have provenance and checksums in `manifest.json`; the viewer's cached sample is explicitly not claimed to be revision-pinned. Large corpus streaming pins the repository revision. All questions from a decoded image share the same image-group split.

The small sample is suitable for pipeline validation. A production tokenizer must be trained on a much larger, representative training-only corpus. The model uses the tokenizer's actual vocabulary size, so parameter count should be rechecked when changing the vocabulary. The nominal production count assumes 32,768 tokens.

To pack a bounded text corpus from public data:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.corpus \
  --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --subset sample-10BT \
  --tokenizer data/production-tokenizer.json \
  --output data/pilot-corpus --max-tokens 1000000
```

`--max-tokens` is mandatory. The packer stops at a document boundary and requires nonempty train and validation splits. It does not download the whole public corpus. Packed 32K-vocabulary tokens occupy about 2 bytes each: 20B tokens need roughly 40GB; 1T tokens need roughly 2TB, before deduplication metadata and checkpoint storage. This instance had about 178GB free at the initial check, so larger runs need streamed data or additional storage.

For a GPU pilot after CUDA access is verified, first benchmark a short run. A four-GPU launch is:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m torch.distributed.run --standalone --nproc_per_node 4 \
  -m slm_lab.training pretrain --config configs/model.json \
  --tokenizer data/production-tokenizer.json --data data/pilot-corpus \
  --output checkpoints/gpu-pilot --device cuda --steps 100 \
  --batch-size 1 --gradient-accumulation 16 --gradient-checkpointing --checkpoint-every 25
```

This is a launch template, not a submitted job. With four workers, 4096-token packed sequences, and 16 accumulation microbatches, each optimizer update supervises approximately 262,080 tokens. The prepared 1M-token sample is only for an initial launch test and would repeat many times in this template; it must be replaced with the intended larger training corpus before a real pilot. Sequence length, global token batch, learning rate, and total steps need tuning from measured throughput and held-out loss before a long run. Full-precision Adam states require approximately 16 bytes per trained parameter before activations; image attention, longer context, reference policies, and reward models add memory. The architecture accepts one image at 224px or 448px, with 49 or 196 pooled visual tokens; fine-detail OCR remains limited. Large-scale image training, adaptive tiling, larger and more representative language/code/math mixtures, a larger context curriculum, and capability benchmarks remain unfinished.

For CPU validation, use `configs/tiny.json`, the public sample tokenizer, and a new output directory:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.training pretrain --config configs/tiny.json \
  --tokenizer data/public-sample/tokenizer.json \
  --data data/public-sample/pretrain.train.jsonl \
  --output checkpoints/new-cpu-run --steps 80 --batch-size 4
```

Subsequent stages use `--checkpoint .../model.pt` and the same tokenizer. SFT records need `prompt`, `response`, and an optional relative `image` path. Preference records need a shared `prompt`, `chosen`, `rejected`, and `feedback_source` set to `human`, `ai`, or `synthetic`. PPO additionally requires `--reward-checkpoint` from the reward stage and verifies that human feedback was used. This distinguishes RLHF from training on synthetic preferences. DPO and PPO are alternative policy-training branches; their short CPU validation runs both started from the SFT checkpoint.

Resume a partial snapshot with `--resume --checkpoint .../step-00000025.pt`, the same data and total step budget, and a new output directory. `model.pt` is the inference/stage-transfer checkpoint; `training-state.pt` and periodic snapshots additionally include the optimizer and fixed alignment reference. Dataset JSONL files support bounded experiments; use packed shards for large text pretraining. Bounded JSONL runs explicitly report any truncated sequences.

Held-out reports are in `reports/`. Text validation measured perplexity 510.38 before training and 220.12 after 80 pretraining steps on the same 12 held-out documents, truncated to 128 tokens. Image-question generation scored 0/11; human-preference validation scored 1/8. Neither result establishes useful multimodal or alignment capability. These particular results belong to the small replica. Full-size first-update checkpoints are in `checkpoints/full-first-pretrain/` and `checkpoints/full-first-sft/`; their weights remain essentially untrained. A two-document full-size held-out check is recorded in `reports/full-first-heldout.json` and is not a broad benchmark.

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.evaluation \
  --checkpoint checkpoints/cpu-pretrain/model.pt \
  --data data/public-sample/pretrain.valid.jsonl \
  --output reports/new-heldout.json
.venv/bin/python -m slm_lab.budget --parameters 706012672 \
  --tokens 20000000000 --sustained-tflops 70 --gpus 4 \
  --hourly-rate 0 --multiplier 1.5
```

`reports/compute-budget.json` contains transparent scenarios. GPU throughput and the 1.5x additional-work multiplier are assumptions, not benchmarks. At an assumed 40–100 sustained TFLOPS per A100, a 20B-token pilot needs roughly 353–883 GPU-hours, or 4–9 days on four cards. At an assumed 100–250 sustained TFLOPS per H100, a 1T-token run needs roughly 7,060–17,650 GPU-hours, or 37–92 days on eight cards. At the [checked Lambda price](https://lambda.ai/instances) of $3.99/H100-hour, that is about $28K–$70K in GPU rental. Image/reward workloads, failed experiments, and poor scaling can exceed the allowance; storage, CPU work, human labeling, and taxes are excluded. Existing A100 scenarios omit their cost rather than treating them as free.

The target of high-end-LLM-level capability remains unachieved and unverified. Further work must measure language knowledge, reasoning, coding, instruction following, image understanding, hallucination, and preference quality under matched inference budgets. See `RESEARCH.md` for the research basis and pending experiments.

The nominal 32K tokenizer prepared here is in `data/tokenizer-corpus/tokenizer.json` and was trained on 1,835 public training documents, with validation documents excluded. It is an initial pilot vocabulary; a broader representative corpus and vocabulary ablation remain necessary. The actual 706M architecture also completed a two-step synthetic text-and-image CPU resource test at 64 text tokens, with about 11.4 GiB peak process RSS. This short-context CPU measurement does not establish GPU memory or throughput at long context. `GPU_REQUEST.md` contains the concrete platform allocation request.


Expanded public-data collection is implemented in `public_sources.py` and `public_books.py`. The collector reads pinned GitHub archives without executing downloaded code, records repository licenses and checksums, excludes generated/vendor files, fetches Wikipedia revisions with attribution history links, and checks Gutenberg books against the primary US public-domain catalog before removing the distribution wrapper. Distill essays supply openly licensed technical writing. Collection is bounded and does not claim to contain all open-source code. Source group IDs keep all chunks of a book, article or code file in one split. Near-duplicate detection across sources and repository-fork grouping remain unfinished.

`configs/public-sources.json` lists the requested domain seeds and initial code projects. Reference domains include medicine, law, software engineering, computers, networking, psychology, astrology, astronomy, physics, mathematics, chemistry, biology, engineering, economics and humanities. The domain labels identify seed/link neighborhoods and are not an expert annotation. Astrology remains a separate label from astronomy.

`code_evaluation.py` checks eight elementary Python function completions, with bounded execution that rejects imports and attribute access. The starting checkpoint passed 0/8. This small check does not establish performance across programming languages or frameworks. The pilot also measures held-out loss by domain and code-file extension. Broader executable multilingual benchmarks and instruction tuning are still needed.


Post-training evaluation in `scripts/posttrain_evaluation.py` verifies the completed training pass before assessing the model. `code_quality.py` separates syntax, hidden-case correctness and empirical runtime/memory stress across Python and JavaScript. The 12 tasks per language include edge cases, randomized cases and inputs up to 32,768 elements. The executor supports a constrained Python subset and host-isolated QuickJS. A separate C++17 assessment is described below; additional languages and repository-level tasks remain pending. Stress tests do not prove optimal asymptotic complexity.

The knowledge assessment reserves 560 balanced MMLU-Pro test questions across 14 subjects. An exact normalized question-substring audit against all 314,276,207 packed training tokens found one match, which was excluded. The 559 retained questions receive both generated-answer and answer-letter-likelihood scores, with chance baselines and confidence intervals. Near-duplicates and semantic overlap are not excluded by this audit. These are local sample results, not a complete standardized benchmark or a matched frontier-model comparison. Status and completed reports are in `reports/posttrain-evaluation-v1/`.

The next-stage data include 9,500 human-written No Robots demonstrations, 12,000 genuinely human-ranked HH preference pairs fetched from the original GitHub repository, and 2,419 source-file-separated Python function exercises in `data/code-functions-v2/`. No Robots carries CC-BY-NC-4.0; preference data and code retain their recorded source licenses. Commons image-caption collection is ongoing with per-image rights, uploader attribution and pixel-group train/validation splits. Training supports freezing the language component for a vision warmup and homogeneous image/text minibatches for joint SFT. None of these prepared next-stage datasets proves that multimodal or human-feedback capability has been achieved.

The main visual curriculum targets ordinary color photos, scene text, document pages, and scientific diagrams. Collection completed for 12,000 training photos and 800 validation photos from [Open Images](https://storage.googleapis.com/openimages/web/download_v7.html), paired with original human descriptions from [Localized Narratives](https://google.github.io/localized-narratives/). The assembled training split retains 11,971 of these photos after held-out-image exclusions. Source photo authors, attribution URLs, image licenses, annotation licenses and complete download hashes are retained. This uses the original Google-hosted annotations and the CVDF public image mirror, without pretrained weights or model-generated captions.

The scene-text collection has 5,000 training photos and 400 validation photos with original [TextOCR](https://textvqa.org/textocr/dataset/) word annotations: 23,831 training and 1,878 validation reading examples, including word crops and whole-scene region questions. The original annotation download returned HTTP 403, so original-format JSON was fetched from a pinned mirror; independent hash equivalence to the blocked endpoint is unverified. The document supplement was collected from actual [GovInfo public legislation PDFs](https://www.govinfo.gov/about/policies). After curriculum exclusions, it retains **157 training pages / 1,382 reading examples** and **21 validation pages / 180 reading examples**, derived from their text layers. Original document groups remain separated. The main curriculum includes an immutable snapshot of licensed Commons captions/diagrams and caps Fashion-MNIST at **500 training examples** as an auxiliary category task. Full Fashion-only files are historical preparation artifacts, not the main training inputs.

`scripts/prepare_alignment_curriculum.py --wait-images` builds the combined curriculum once collection finishes. It excludes every known official held-out photo ID across sources, recomputes the exact model-input pixel hashes at **224px and 448px**, and excludes an entire training photo group when any crop matches a held-out crop. It also filters exact reserved knowledge questions. The prepared curriculum contains **38,032 training and 3,093 validation examples**, with zero exact pixel/photo-group overlap. Perceptual near-duplicates remain unaudited. Oversized PNG color/text profiles were stripped while checking that every modified image retained its exact 224px model-input pixels; original download bytes, checksums, authors and licenses remain recorded. Region-reading prompts map original boxes into the actual padded input coordinates.

`scripts/train_multimodal_pilot.py --wait` follows the completed text pilot and its post-training evaluation. All four training stages have completed: instruction SFT, own-weight contrastive alignment, image-reading SFT with corrected box coordinates, and joint SFT. Earlier image-reading updates using original-image boxes are retained as a superseded prototype. Joint SFT used **448px inputs/196 visual tokens**, interpolating the learned 224px spatial grid without changing the **706,012,672 parameter** count. The approach follows [ViT's resolution interpolation](https://arxiv.org/html/2010.11929v2). The full-size L40 forward/backward check is in `reports/l40-high-resolution-readiness.json`; it uses synthetic tensors, performs no optimizer updates, and does not measure OCR ability. Evaluation at 448px produced only **1/170 exact OCR answers** and **2/128 correct image-to-caption retrievals** (chance is 1/128). These results do not establish reliable image understanding or reading. Code and knowledge evaluation follow the completed visual checks; live status is in `reports/multimodal-pilot-v1/status.json`.

Fashion-MNIST is an auxiliary clothing-category task, capped at **500/38,032 training image examples (1.31%)**. The remaining examples cover ordinary photos, scene-text reading, document reading, and Commons captions. Reading examples include several crops from the same original photo/page; the 38,032 examples represent **17,829 original image groups**, not 38,032 independent photos. See `reports/image-curriculum-audit.json` for the verified source counts and training/evaluation snapshot. Dense small text, handwriting, charts and broad visual reasoning still require further work.

The completed visual pilot also passed 0/24 functional code tasks in both completion and instruction formats; syntax passed 1/24 and 3/24 respectively. Generated knowledge accuracy was 3.22% and answer-letter likelihood accuracy was 12.16%, versus 11.18% chance (the likelihood accuracy interval includes chance). These results are in `reports/multimodal-pilot-v1/`. A bounded OCR diagnostic compared the image-reading and joint checkpoints on identical 32-example samples from training and validation; neither checkpoint produced an exact answer on either split. This points to underfitting, rather than establishing good training-set reading with poor generalization. See `reports/visual-learning-diagnostics-v1/summary.json`.

`slm_lab.rendered_text` prepared a **50,000-training/2,000-validation lettering supplement**, with programmatically sampled characters/number strings and words from the previously collected public-domain Gutenberg books. Exact rendering labels, source attribution, Liberation font hashes/notices, image hashes and generation seeds are retained. Text groups are separated after case/whitespace normalization; validation also withholds two font styles, and training excludes every recorded real OCR validation answer. These are synthetic lettering crops, not real scenes or human preference feedback. Inspired by [synthetic OCR training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.09313), `scripts/train_lettering_experiment.py` completed vision warmup, joint reading and matched evaluation, retaining all **38,032 existing real/auxiliary image examples** and the text demonstrations. The candidate answered **0/170 real OCR examples exactly** and failed the reading-gain/code-syntax selection guards; it was **not promoted**. The longer curriculum starts from the original DPO policy. Selection checks are tuning heuristics, not independent final tests or a capability certificate. The longer curriculum retains 10,000 lettering examples in later visual stages; reliable reading remains unachieved. See `reports/lettering-experiment-v1/selection.json`.

The first text pass completed 1,199 updates/314,233,920 target tokens. Held-out code NLL fell from 10.345 to 1.570; reference-domain NLL fell from 10.011 to 4.202. Functional code generation nevertheless passed **0/24** local Python/JavaScript tasks in both completion and instruction formats. The instruction format produced valid syntax on 6/24 tasks. On 559 reserved MMLU-Pro questions, forced-choice letter likelihood reached **12.88%**, versus **11.18% chance** (95% accuracy interval includes chance); generated-answer accuracy was **0.72%**. These are weak baseline capabilities, not a successful general model. See `reports/posttrain-evaluation-v1/summary.json` and `code-quality-instruction.json`. Code evaluation now preserves separate completion/instruction results and tests the same hidden cases for both.

`scripts/train_human_feedback_pilot.py` follows completed multimodal training/evaluation. It prepares separate reward and DPO branches from the same own multimodal SFT policy, using 12,000 original human-ranked HH training pairs and 1,000 held-out pairs with all original prompts/completions preserved. Exact reserved knowledge questions and source prompt overlap are checked. PPO is a separate branch and starts only if at least 500 full held-out human pairs give a 95% ranking interval above chance. Offline ranking does not prove on-policy reward fidelity; fresh human policy comparisons remain necessary. PPO stores probabilities actually used during generation and normalizes advantages over the rollout batch. CPU replica resume tests matched uninterrupted training exactly; this is mechanics evidence, not full-model RLHF success.

Reward training (1,500 updates) and DPO (3,000 updates) have completed. A matched numerical probe found that BF16 rounding in the scalar reward projection created 69 ties: ranking accuracy was 50.5%, while an FP32 projection on identical backbone activations scored **53.8% with zero ties** (95% interval 50.70–56.87%). Forward inference, cached generation and training now keep this small projection in FP32. The original BF16-head reports remain archived alongside the verified FP32 evaluation. DPO finished before its scheduler was reloaded; no DPO updates were interrupted. The corrected predefined gate passed, and the **128-update full-model PPO experiment completed training and evaluation**. This is a modest offline ranking result, not proven own-policy reward quality or successful alignment. See `reports/human-feedback-pilot-v1/reward-precision-probe.json`, `reward-ranking-fp32-verified.json`, `reward-gate.json` and `precision-transition.json`.

`slm_lab.object_qa` prepared **8,205 training / 763 validation questions** from the original [Open Images object annotations](https://storage.googleapis.com/openimages/web/factsfigures_v6.html), reusing photos already admitted by the image-group/pixel audit. The training tasks contain 6,971 region identifications, 949 class-instance counts and 285 spatial questions. Original image attribution remains attached; publisher CRC32C and local SHA256 verify the annotation files. Boxes follow the documented photo rotations, then the actual input letterbox coordinates. Crowd/uncertain classes, conflicting duplicate boxes, ambiguous identical class regions and targets below 24 pixels at 448px are excluded. Questions and answers are programmatically derived from public annotations, not human-written VQA or human preference labels. An untrained prototype before the size filter is retained separately.

`slm_lab.object_evaluation` measures region/count/spatial answers with blank and wrong-image controls and reports exact specific-class/count/yes-no targets. These introductory assessments reuse the existing caption validation photo groups; they are tuning evidence, not independent final tests or broad visual reasoning proof. The extended pipeline records a baseline before training, retains this QA curriculum in its visual/joint stages, and evaluates it afterwards. New images, charts, documents, multilingual OCR and independent final tests remain necessary.

The larger language/code expansion is now collecting a **2B-token budget** from the completed [October 2026 Wikimedia article snapshot](https://dumps.wikimedia.org/enwiki/20261001/) and up to **1B tokens** of documented code from [CodeSearchNet](https://github.com/github/CodeSearchNet), across Python, JavaScript, Java, Go, PHP and Ruby. These are requested budgets, not completed volume. Wikipedia source parts are checked against the publisher's SHA1 and retained SHA256; article revisions, attribution/history links and token offsets are recorded. Complete parts resume independently. Reference sections/inline citations are removed before packing, mathematics is retained where supported, and canonical page IDs keep previous held-out pages out of training. Domain hints from titles/categories are approximate. Wikipedia text is licensed CC-BY-SA data, not public-domain text or a guarantee of factual accuracy.

The original CodeSearchNet S3 download returned HTTP 403. Original ZIP files and their supplied per-repository license catalogues are available from a pinned pre-conversion mirror, verified against its Git-LFS hashes; equivalence to the blocked origin is not independently established. Legacy catalogues are decoded with a primitive-opcode whitelist and a global/constructor-denying reader. Only recognized closest-ancestor license notices are accepted, and conflicting/unknown notices are excluded. Original notices, URLs, code hashes, official partitions and source-file groups remain recorded. All official validation/test repositories across the six languages are protected; files already used in the first pilot are excluded from new validation. Exact source-code duplicates and reserved knowledge-question matches are filtered. New instruction responses use source comments and implementations, with repeated Python docstrings removed; imports/class context, correctness and current API compatibility are unverified. Source code is never executed during collection.

`scripts/train_extended_curriculum.py` now starts foundation learning while the remaining Wikipedia collection continues. `slm_lab.snapshot_corpus` freezes only committed source parts, verifies provenance/shard hashes, preserves held-out splits and uses symlinks to avoid copying token storage. The fixed snapshot contains **730,174,765 Wikipedia training tokens**; the completed code corpus contributes **524,007,604 training tokens**. The four-L40 warmup is running for **4,785 updates / 1,254,052,800 target-token exposures**, approximately one pass through this mixture. It starts from the original DPO policy retained by the lettering experiment. Source collection never appends data to this frozen training view. The warmup tokens recur in the eventual full corpus, so later passes are repeated exposure, not additional unique data.

After warmup and full Wikipedia collection, the extended pipeline performs two passes through the larger language/code mixture, multilingual code instruction training, verified standalone code, math reasoning, vision realignment, image-reading SFT and joint SFT. Completed models receive separate completion/instruction code checks, numeric math answers, domain knowledge, visual grounding and OCR evaluation. A subsequent human-feedback pipeline is queued for this expanded policy. These stages still need measured capability gains and matched comparisons; completion will not itself establish the requested high-end-model capability. Live state is in `reports/extended-curriculum-v1/status.json`, with the fixed first-stage budget in `foundation-warmup-plan.json`.

The first DPO object-question baseline answered **0/164** sampled questions exactly. Wrong-image likelihood was almost indistinguishable from correct-image likelihood (gain 0.0082 NLL), despite a much larger gain over blank images. This suggests weak specific-image grounding, rather than useful object/count/spatial reasoning. The raw generations and per-task controls are in `reports/human-feedback-pilot-v1/dpo-object-baseline.json`; annotated QA training remains queued.

The coding supplement now contains **673 distinct source programs / 2,692 checked name variants** from Google's original [MBPP training split](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-research/google-research/f46ca8374b4cddef97ca4208ad986049d74d296a/mbpp/README.md), licensed training-file functions and CodeSearchNet comments. Original literal examples/tests pass in the constrained Python worker; tuples retain their Python types through literal data parsing. No arbitrary test expressions are evaluated. Unsupported imports/classes, malformed examples and failing programs are excluded. All MBPP prompt/validation/test IDs (1–600), raw and sanitized reference hashes, and exact local benchmark specifications are excluded from this supplement. Name variants preserve the group and repeat published cases; they are extra training exposures, not new algorithms. Passing these examples does not prove general correctness or efficiency. The frozen training view is in `data/verified-code-curriculum-v1/`.

The extended pipeline adds a two-pass standalone-code stage after multilingual code instruction training, retaining No Robots demonstrations and a multilingual code sample. It records three copies of each verified row separately from the distinct-program count, and retains one copy during final joint visual training. These stages are queued, not completed. A new public-programming assessment supports **175/257 sanitized MBPP test problems**, with 82 explicit exclusions for the constrained runner/reference checks. Only the specification/signature reaches the model. This is a zero-shot supported-subset assessment, not the standard full MBPP benchmark or contamination-certified independent test; prior pretraining corpora are not fully audited for its references. It runs after extended training alongside the private syntax/hidden-case/efficiency suite. Other languages, dependency/API use and repository edits remain necessary.

The completed DPO and PPO branches each still passed **0/24 functional coding tasks** in both prompt formats and **1/170 OCR examples** exactly. Domain answer-letter likelihood was 12.34% (DPO) and 12.16% (PPO), versus 11.18% chance. These weak results support continuing the larger foundation and verified-code stages; completing human-feedback mechanics has not produced a capable general model. The lettering experiment has completed and was not promoted.

`slm_lab.cpp_evaluation` adds **12 C++17 function tasks**, separating compilation, hidden-case correctness and reference-relative runtime/memory stress. `scripts/install_native_toolchain.py` installs the hash-verified official Zig compiler. The compile and runtime launchers apply Linux Landlock and seccomp before executing candidate code, including static initializers; guard setup failures stop evaluation. Runtime access to private files, networking, subprocesses and other processes is restricted, with CPU/memory/output/time limits. These are kernel restrictions, not a separate virtual machine. The reference implementations passed **1,724 hidden cases** and all stress runs; the **trained DPO policy passed 0/12 syntax, correctness and efficiency tasks**. Runner readiness is separate from model quality. Parent-measured timings include input transport and are kept separate from Python/JavaScript scores; empirical stress cannot prove asymptotic optimality. The extended pipeline runs this assessment after training. Reports are in `reports/cpp-reference-readiness.json` and `reports/human-feedback-pilot-v1/dpo-cpp-baseline.json`. The full suite passed **151 tests**, including native-worker, arithmetic-parser, frozen-source integrity, feedback-selection, frozen-visual-probe and CTC loss/decoding checks; broader languages, library use and repository work remain unverified.

`slm_lab.math_corpus` prepares original human-written [GSM8K solutions](https://github.com/openai/grade-school-math) directly from the author's GitHub repository, pinned to revision `3101c7d5072418e28b9008a6636bde82a006892c` with expected file hashes and the MIT notice. It retains **6,991 training / 387 validation problems**, checking **23,716 published arithmetic annotations** using bounded rational AST interpretation. Decimal annotations permit only half their displayed rounding unit; integer annotations must match exactly. The 95 original training rows without calculation annotations are recorded as exclusions. Calculator annotations are removed from the retained prose; no teacher weights, generated Socratic questions or model-written solutions are used. All **1,319 official test problems** remain separate. The extended math stage trains two passes with instruction/code retention, and final joint training retains the math problems.

`slm_lab.math_evaluation` shows only questions to the model, uses fixed greedy generation without tools or repair, and scores the final `####` numeric line exactly. The original DPO policy scored **0/64 correct, with zero numeric-format compliance**, on the fixed hash-ordered pilot sample; raw responses are in `reports/human-feedback-pilot-v1/dpo-math-baseline.json`. This is a bounded tuning sample, not a full benchmark score. The longer pipeline evaluates the whole official test split. This zero-shot protocol differs from publisher few-shot/calculator experiments; earlier complete pretraining corpora are not contamination-certified, and correct final numbers do not prove valid reasoning or advanced mathematical competence. The active prepared data are in `data/math-reasoning-v2/`; the earlier decimal-parser preparation is an unused prototype.

To preserve space for ongoing training, final optimizer/reference resume states from the three completed original human-feedback pilots were removed after checking their metadata against the retained models and training reports. This reclaimed **28.7 GiB**; all learned weights and evaluations remain. These old pilots can be used for inference/comparison, but their removed optimizer states cannot resume the exact original optimization history. The audit is `reports/checkpoint-storage-cleanup-v2.json`.

The extended human-feedback scheduler now runs with `--capability-guards`. Before reward/DPO/PPO training it obtains matched evidence for the exact pre-feedback policy, reusing the completed extended assessment when source/runtime/data fingerprints match. Both feedback branches then receive all nine declared assessments: completion and instruction coding, C++, supported public programming, full official math test coverage, subject knowledge, caption grounding, OCR and object questions. `slm_lab.alignment_selection` checks each language/domain, preserves previously correct local functions, and checks the visual tasks separately. An eligible branch must also show positive held-out human-pair likelihood improvement with a lower 95% positive-margin bound above one half on at least 500 pairs. The base policy is retained if no branch passes; selection is recorded in `reports/extended-human-feedback-v1/selection.json` after those stages finish.

These are predefined local tuning heuristics, not proof of broad ability or fresh human preference for generated responses. `reports/human-feedback-regression-diagnostic.json` applies a partial retrospective check to the original pilots: DPO lost reported instruction-code syntax, one subject score, caption metrics and reading error; PPO lost instruction-code syntax and did not clear the preference-improvement checks. Those historical reports lack the newer source/pixel metadata and assessments, so they cannot select a policy under the full new rule.

New evaluation reports record implementation hashes, Python/package versions, tokenizer, precision, generation budgets and actual image-input fingerprints. A source/runtime or dataset change during evaluation prevents writing a sealed report, and mismatched cases, pixels, source versions or budgets prevent selection. A tiny CPU inference check verifies report instrumentation only; it is not nominal-model capability evidence. The active foundation workers continue using their loaded training implementation.

The completed lettering policy also answered **0/256 synthetic word-validation examples** exactly. `slm_lab.visual_character_probe` therefore tests frozen visual features with a separate diagnostic classifier. It renders **1,736 training / 744 validation single-glyph images**, balances all 62 ASCII letter/digit classes, withholds two font styles, verifies the public font hashes/notices and checks zero exact input overlap. A fixed ridge classifier fits only training glyphs on a coarse 2x2 pre-projection feature grid. On the held-out styles it scored **23.4%** for the original DPO encoder, **33.1%** after lettering warmup, **31.0%** after joint reading, and **22.2%** for matched random weights. Blank-image accuracy was **1.6%**. Encoder state hashes remained unchanged throughout; the fitted diagnostic heads are never deployed or transferred. This shows a limited character-feature gain, not word/scene/document reading. Coarse pooling and linear-classifier underfitting prevent a definitive attribution of all OCR failure. Results are in `reports/visual-character-probe-v1/summary.json`.

`slm_lab.character_ctc` implements a character-sequence experiment using the existing own vision encoder/projector and **fixed character prototypes from our own language embedding**. It reads unpooled spatial features, averages height into columns, interpolates 56 alignment frames and computes FP32 CTC loss with explicit repeated-character/time checks. It adds **zero trainable parameters**; the deployed configuration remains **706,012,672 parameters**. The CUDA forward/backward check verifies finite patch/projector gradients without optimizer updates, separately from learning results (`reports/character-sequence-readiness.json`).

The sequence curriculum contains **68,030 training transcripts** (50,000 rendered / 18,030 real-word crops) and **3,429 validation transcripts** (2,000 rendered / 1,429 real). It keeps original provenance, separates parent-image groups and excludes synthetic validation word groups from this objective's training data. Unsupported non-ASCII/empty transcripts, one time-limit case and 183 protected-word matches are recorded. Real-image word vocabulary can occur on both photo splits; previous training may also have encountered these development targets. `checkpoints/character-sequence-pilot-v1` completed a **one-pass / 2,126-update** vision-only pilot on GPU 3, sharing the existing allocation with text training during that run. The one-pass pilot completed, and both its initial and final greedy readouts scored **0/256 exact words**. The final readout produced **252 empty strings**, despite a lower training loss. A frozen bounded prefix-probability diagnostic also scored **0/128 exact words** (character error 0.961), using no lexicon or reference-word access. This objective has not been promoted into the production policy. Component CTC reading is separate from the language model's native autoregressive transcription; neither native multimodal improvement nor broad vision ability is established by a lower auxiliary loss.

The first foundation checkpoint (1,000 updates / **262,080,000 new training targets**) received a matched 64-window validation assessment per component. Held-out code NLL improved **1.608 → 1.048**, and Wikipedia NLL **4.723 → 3.529**. The data, sample positions, tokenizer, runtime and evaluator hashes match between the original DPO policy and this snapshot. This is token-prediction learning evidence, not correct-program, reasoning or high-end-model evidence. Matched code checks on that snapshot still passed **0/24 functional and efficiency tasks** in both formats. Instruction syntax improved **0/24 → 14/24**; completion syntax changed **9/24 → 8/24**. This is formatting progress without demonstrated algorithmic correctness. See `reports/foundation-step1000-eval-v1/loss-comparison.json`. The nominal foundation pass still has 4,785 planned updates.

The next readout experiment is `slm_lab.learned_character_ctc`: learned height attention, local temporal convolutions/upsampling and an unconstrained character classifier on the existing own encoder. The head adds **877,921 parameters**, for **706,890,593 combined deployed parameters** if later adopted. It keeps the existing language projector frozen because this readout does not use it. A full-size frozen-feature sanity check fitted **8/8 training words** with the head only; this is memorization/learning-path verification, not held-out OCR evidence (`reports/learned-character-readiness.json`). The actual experiment runs **three passes / 6,378 updates** over the same audited 68,030 transcripts, first warming the head for 200 updates, then fine-tuning the encoder at one tenth of the head rate. It shares GPU 3 with the ongoing text run and has not been promoted. Held-out reading controls and native language/photo/code/knowledge preservation remain required.

`slm_lab.sequence_diagnostics` freezes a single atomic checkpoint with a temporary hard link and reports image controls, CTC blank behavior, exact words and character error on both training and validation cases. It reconstructs the audited trainer's deterministic order to distinguish already-exposed training examples from not-yet-exposed ones. At **1,100 updates**, the learned readout had seen 35,200 of 68,030 training rows, with encoder updates on 28,800. It scored **0/24 already-exposed sampled training words** and **0/64 validation words** (32 real / 32 rendered); validation blank argmax occupied 98.3% of frames. Loss sensitivity to proper versus wrong images does not establish correct reading. The full three-pass endpoint remains pending. Report: `reports/learned-character-interim-v2.json`; the earlier update-800 report lacks exposure accounting and must not be interpreted as a fully exposed training-set fitting check.

`scripts/train_verified_code_probe.py` starts a separate, bounded code-SFT intervention from the own **foundation update-1,000 snapshot**, preserving the main four-worker run. Its weights-only source is verified tensor-for-tensor against the saved foundation state. The frozen curriculum contains **673 original public programs / 2,692 checked name variants**; all examples fit without truncation (maximum 1,952 tokens). The declared intervention is **two passes / 673 updates**, with microbatch 2, accumulation 4 and learning rate 1e-5, sharing physical GPU 2. This is 5,384 variant-row exposures and 832,152 supervised targets, not 5,384 independent algorithms. Fresh completion/instruction coding checks run before and after under matching evaluator fingerprints and generation budgets, separating syntax, hidden-case correctness and runtime/memory stress. It has no automatic production promotion and cannot establish broad coding/general capability. Live plan/status: `reports/verified-code-foundation-probe-v1/`.

The verified-code intervention completed all **673 updates** without truncation. On matched 24-task checks, completion syntax improved from **8/24 to 15/24**, while instruction syntax fell from **14/24 to 9/24**. **Functional correctness and efficiency remained 0/24 in both formats**. The candidate has not been promoted; its supervised final-batch loss of 0.169 does not establish algorithmic skill. Results: `reports/verified-code-foundation-probe-v1/comparison.json`. Broader foundation learning and instruction/multilingual retention remain necessary.

The completed Commons collection now contains **1,475 images** across 15 subject groups (1,335 training / 140 validation). `slm_lab.visual_pretraining_data` freezes **18,316 training / 1,361 validation full images** from the earlier real-photo, scene-text and document sources plus the completed Commons collection. It uses one full image per parent, preferring captions when a photo also has scene-text tasks, and includes **no Fashion-MNIST or rendered lettering images**. Training contains 11,971 ordinary photos, 4,853 additional full text scenes, 157 pages and 1,335 Commons images. Exact parent/pixel overlap at 224px and 448px is zero; near-duplicates remain unaudited. Source authors, licenses, hashes and original annotations remain in the records. The earlier live curriculum stays frozen.

`slm_lab.masked_visual_pretraining` adds an adapted [MAE-style image-only objective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.06377): the existing own encoder sees 49 of 196 patches at 224px; a **2,084,352-parameter training-only decoder** reconstructs the hidden normalized RGB patches. The deployed model would retain **706,012,672 parameters** when this decoder is discarded. The unused language projector stays frozen; reconstruction does not use captions, OCR transcripts or external teacher weights. Random resized crops augment training, with horizontal flips only for ordinary photos. The full-size 32-real-photo CUDA check completed two temporary optimizer updates with finite encoder/decoder gradients and about 1.05 GiB peak allocation; those temporary weights were discarded. This is resource/learning-mechanics evidence, not semantic capability (`reports/masked-visual-readiness-v1.json`).

The actual masked-vision run is queued behind the verified live reading-process identity on existing GPU 3. Its bounded budget is **10 passes / 5,724 updates**, including 183,168 image exposures (eight padding repetitions). The scheduler checks loaded implementation hashes before starting; it does not stop the main four-worker foundation run. Fixed-mask reconstruction checks run before/after.
